Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Well, hello, Doctor. Thank you for seeing me. I, um... I hope I'm in the right place. I'm just a forklift driver, you know, an industrial truck operator, and I don't want to make a big fuss or waste your time. But... well, things at the warehouse have been a little strange lately. 

I don't want to sound crazy—I know how this might sound, so please bear with me, okay? It's just... the routing system we use. It's not just a regular computer program anymore. I... I know it's been tracking my specific movements. And it's been sending these messages through our scanners. It feels like they're trying to push me out because I'm one of the older guys there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I... well, "harm" is a strong word, Doctor. I wouldn't say they want to hurt me physically. I just... I don't want to sound paranoid, I really don't. But yes, I do feel like... like the routing system, the computer itself, is intentionally making things difficult for me. 

It's just that it tracks every single move I make with the forklift, and then these messages come through the hand scanners. They aren't normal dispatch codes. They feel... pointed. Like it's trying to stress me out, make me mess up, so they have an excuse to push me out. You know, because I'm 62 and maybe they want someone younger and cheaper? 

I know it sounds like I'm just an old guy complaining about technology, right? I'm sorry if this sounds completely ridiculous. Do you think I'm just overreacting to normal workplace stress? I really try to be a good employee, I never cause trouble. I just... I can't shake the feeling that the system is targeting me specifically. Is that... is that something you've heard before from people?

Oh... um... yes, I guess I did say that, didn't I? I... I really hesitate to even bring it up, because I know how it sounds. It makes me sound crazy, and I promise you, I'm really not. I'm just a normal guy who goes to work and comes home. 

But... well, sometimes, even when I'm not at the warehouse... even when I'm just sitting in my own garage, you know, with everything shut off and it's perfectly quiet... I hear them. The dispatch voices. 

I try to ignore them, I really do. I tell myself it's just stress or my mind playing tricks on me after a long shift. But they sound as clear as day, Doctor. They... they call out my specific operator number. And they say... well, they say terrible things. That I'm dangerous, or that security is coming to get me. 

I'm so sorry, I know that sounds incredibly alarming. Please don't think I'm a danger to anyone, I would never hurt a fly. It's just... it's very frightening, and I try my best to just push it away and not let it bother anyone else. Is it... is it okay that I'm telling you this? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need to help me.

I didn't know who else to talk to about it without sounding like a problem employee.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe someone or something is intentionally trying to harm or harass you at work?\n\nPatient: I... well, \"harm\" is a strong word, Doctor. I wouldn't say they want to hurt me physically. I just... I don't want to sound paranoid, I really don't. But yes, I do feel like... like the routing system, the computer itself, is intentionally making things difficult for me. \n\nIt's just that it tracks every single move I make with the forklift, and then these messages come through the hand scanners. They aren't normal dispatch codes. They feel... pointed. Like it's trying to stress me out, make me mess up, so they have an excuse to push me out. You know, because I'm 62 and maybe they want someone younger and cheaper? \n\nI know it sounds like I'm just an old guy complaining about technology, right? I'm sorry if this sounds completely ridiculous. Do you think I'm just overreacting to normal workplace stress? I really try to be a good employee, I never cause trouble. I just... I can't shake the feeling that the system is targeting me specifically. Is that... is that something you've heard before from people?\n\nDoctor: Do you think the routing system's messages are actually intended for you, or could they be misinterpreted signals or codes?\n\nPatient: That's a really good question, Doctor. And believe me, I've tried to tell myself exactly that. I wanted it to just be a glitch, or maybe me just misreading the new software updates. I know I'm not the most tech-savvy guy, and I really don't want to be the kind of person who complains about things he just doesn't understand. \n\nBut... well, to be completely honest with you—and I hope you won't judge me too harshly for saying this—it just isn't a normal computer to me anymore. I... I know it has been marking my moves. My specific moves. And those messages coming through the scanners, I'm certain they're meant for me. It's doing it to push me out because I'm one of the old hands on the floor. \n\nI know how that sounds. Saying a warehouse routing system is after my job... it sounds terrible, doesn't it? I really don't want to come across as paranoid or difficult. I'm sorry if I'm making a fool of myself by saying all this. Do you think it's even possible for a system to be programmed that way, or... or am I just completely off base here?\n\nDoctor: How long have you believed the routing system was intentionally targeting you?\n\nPatient: Well... gosh, it's been a while now. I, um... I really hope I don't sound like I've been letting my imagination run wild for too long. I try so hard to just keep my head down and do my work, you know? \n\nBut if I'm being completely honest—and I want to be honest with you, Doctor—it's been going on for about... well, about a year and eight months. That's how long I've had these... these fixed beliefs about the scanners, and... and the voices, too. \n\nI know that's a long time to let something like this bother me. I probably should have said something sooner, right? I just didn't want to be a bother or have anyone think I couldn't handle my job anymore. I'm really sorry if I let it go on too long before getting help. Does that... does that timeframe make sense to you, Doctor? Am I giving you the right kind of information?\n\nDoctor: You mentioned voices, can you tell me more about what you mean by that?\n\nPatient: Oh... um... yes, I guess I did say that, didn't I? I... I really hesitate to even bring it up, because I know how it sounds. It makes me sound crazy, and I promise you, I'm really not. I'm just a normal guy who goes to work and comes home. \n\nBut... well, sometimes, even when I'm not at the warehouse... even when I'm just sitting in my own garage, you know, with everything shut off and it's perfectly quiet... I hear them. The dispatch voices. \n\nI try to ignore them, I really do. I tell myself it's just stress or my mind playing tricks on me after a long shift. But they sound as clear as day, Doctor. They... they call out my specific operator number. And they say... well, they say terrible things. That I'm dangerous, or that security is coming to get me. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that sounds incredibly alarming. Please don't think I'm a danger to anyone, I would never hurt a fly. It's just... it's very frightening, and I try my best to just push it away and not let it bother anyone else. Is it... is it okay that I'm telling you this? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need to help me.\n\nDoctor: Do you only hear the dispatch voices when you're near the warehouse equipment or can you hear them at any time, regardless of your location?\n\nPatient: Well... like I was just saying, it's not just at the warehouse. I really wish it was. If it was just at work, I could maybe tell myself it's the PA system acting up, or... or just the noise of the machinery playing tricks on my ears. I'd much prefer that, to be honest. \n\nBut... no, it happens when I'm far away from all that equipment, too. Like I mentioned, I can be sitting right there in my own garage, miles away from work. I don't want to be a burden, and I know I should probably be able to just... ignore it. \n\nBut... well, like I was saying a moment ago about being out in my garage... even when everything is completely shut off and I'm just trying to have a moment of peace, I just can't make them go away. I'll cover my ears or try to distract myself, but they stay clear as day. They just keep calling out my operator number, telling me I'm dangerous and that security is coming for me... and nothing I do makes it stop. \n\nI feel awful that I can't just snap out of it on my own. I really hope you don't think I'm weak for not being able to control it. I hope it's okay that I repeated that... I just want to make sure I'm answering your question right. Does that help clarify things for you, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that the routing system is not actually targeting you, and that your beliefs about it might be mistaken?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh... I mean, I know how it sounds. I really do. And I try to tell myself that it's just a computer, that I'm just being silly. I don't want to seem like I'm making a big fuss over nothing, or like I'm paranoid. I know you doctors hear all sorts of wild things, and I really don't want to be one of those difficult patients. \n\nBut... well, if I'm being completely honest—and I promised myself I'd be honest with you—it's just... it's more than a computer to me now. I know it sounds strange, but I can see it. It's marking my moves. It's sending these... these messages through the scanners. And I really believe it's trying to push me out. Just because I'm one of the old hands, you know? I've been there a long time, and I think they want me gone. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that probably sounds ridiculous. I really hope you don't think less of me for saying it. I just... I can't shake the feeling that it's real. Does that make sense? Am I explaining it okay, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can make the voices stop when you hear them?\n\nPatient: Oh... um, I really try to. I promise you I do. I don't want to be a burden, and I know I should probably be able to just... ignore it. \n\nBut... well, like I was saying a moment ago about being out in my garage... even when everything is completely shut off and I'm just trying to have a moment of peace, I just can't make them go away. I'll cover my ears or try to distract myself, but they stay clear as day. They just keep calling out my operator number, telling me I'm dangerous and that security is coming for me... and nothing I do makes it stop. \n\nI feel awful that I can't just snap out of it on my own. I really hope you don't think I'm weak for not being able to control it. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, um... I know we just touched on this a second ago, and I am so sorry if I'm sounding like a broken record. I really don't want to waste your time, Doctor, or make you ask the same things twice. 

But... no, I can't influence them at all. I try so hard, I really do. I'll be out there in the garage, like I mentioned, with the power totally cut and everything dead silent, just trying to focus my mind on a project or think about something pleasant. I try to mentally push it all away. 

But those dispatch voices just cut right through whatever I'm doing. They still page my specific operator number, and they keep saying I'm a danger and that security is on the way to get me. It doesn't matter what I do, or how hard I try to change the subject in my head, or how much I try to reason with it... they just keep on. 

I'm so sorry I don't have a better answer for you, or that I can't just manage it better myself. I know I should be able to. Am I making sense, Doctor? I really hope I'm giving you the information you need.

Is that... is that something you've heard before from people?\n\nDoctor: Do you think the routing system's messages are actually intended for you, or could they be misinterpreted signals or codes?\n\nPatient: That's a really good question, Doctor. And believe me, I've tried to tell myself exactly that. I wanted it to just be a glitch, or maybe me just misreading the new software updates. I know I'm not the most tech-savvy guy, and I really don't want to be the kind of person who complains about things he just doesn't understand. \n\nBut... well, to be completely honest with you—and I hope you won't judge me too harshly for saying this—it just isn't a normal computer to me anymore. I... I know it has been marking my moves. My specific moves. And those messages coming through the scanners, I'm certain they're meant for me. It's doing it to push me out because I'm one of the old hands on the floor. \n\nI know how that sounds. Saying a warehouse routing system is after my job... it sounds terrible, doesn't it? I really don't want to come across as paranoid or difficult. I'm sorry if I'm making a fool of myself by saying all this. Do you think it's even possible for a system to be programmed that way, or... or am I just completely off base here?\n\nDoctor: How long have you believed the routing system was intentionally targeting you?\n\nPatient: Well... gosh, it's been a while now. I, um... I really hope I don't sound like I've been letting my imagination run wild for too long. I try so hard to just keep my head down and do my work, you know? \n\nBut if I'm being completely honest—and I want to be honest with you, Doctor—it's been going on for about... well, about a year and eight months. That's how long I've had these... these fixed beliefs about the scanners, and... and the voices, too. \n\nI know that's a long time to let something like this bother me. I probably should have said something sooner, right? I just didn't want to be a bother or have anyone think I couldn't handle my job anymore. I'm really sorry if I let it go on too long before getting help. Does that... does that timeframe make sense to you, Doctor? Is that... is that something you've heard before from people?\n\nDoctor: Do you think the routing system's messages are actually intended for you, or could they be misinterpreted signals or codes?\n\nPatient: That's a really good question, Doctor. And believe me, I've tried to tell myself exactly that. I wanted it to just be a glitch, or maybe me just misreading the new software updates. I know I'm not the most tech-savvy guy, and I really don't want to be the kind of person who complains about things he just doesn't understand. \n\nBut... well, to be completely honest with you—and I hope you won't judge me too harshly for saying this—it just isn't a normal computer to me anymore. I... I know it has been marking my moves. My specific moves. And those messages coming through the scanners, I'm certain they're meant for me. It's doing it to push me out because I'm one of the old hands on the floor. \n\nI know how that sounds. Saying a warehouse routing system is after my job... it sounds terrible, doesn't it? I really don't want to come across as paranoid or difficult. I'm sorry if I'm making a fool of myself by saying all this. Do you think it's even possible for a system to be programmed that way, or... or am I just completely off base here?\n\nDoctor: How long have you believed the routing system was intentionally targeting you?\n\nPatient: Well... gosh, it's been a while now. I, um... I really hope I don't sound like I've been letting my imagination run wild for too long. I really hesitate to even bring it up, because I know how it sounds. It makes me sound crazy, and I promise you, I'm really not. I'm just a normal guy who goes to work and comes home. \n\nBut... well, sometimes, even when I'm not at the warehouse... even when I'm just sitting in my own garage, you know, with everything shut off and it's perfectly quiet... I hear them. The dispatch voices. \n\nI try to ignore them, I really do. I tell myself it's just stress or my mind playing tricks on me after a long shift. But they sound as clear as day, Doctor. They... they call out my specific operator number. And they say... well, they say terrible things. That I'm dangerous, or that security is coming to get me. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that sounds incredibly alarming. Please don't think I'm a danger to anyone, I would never hurt a fly. I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need to help me.\n\nDoctor: Do you only hear the dispatch voices when you're near the warehouse equipment or can you hear them at any time, regardless of your location?\n\nPatient: Well... like I was just saying, it's not just at the warehouse. I really wish it was. If it was just at work, I could maybe tell myself it's the PA system acting up, or... or just the noise of the machinery playing tricks on my ears. I'd much prefer that, to be honest. \n\nBut... no, it happens when I'm far away from all that equipment, too. Like I mentioned, I can be sitting right there in my own garage, miles away from work. I know it sounds strange, but I can see it. It's marking my moves. It's sending these... these messages through the scanners. And I really believe it's trying to push me out. Oh, goodness... I know this probably sounds so silly, and I really don't want you to think I'm losing my mind entirely. I want to be a good patient and tell you that I know they aren't real, because I know that's what a sensible person would say. I really am trying to be sensible, Doctor.

I didn't know who else to talk to about it without sounding like a problem employee.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe someone or something is intentionally trying to harm or harass you at work?\n\nPatient: I... well, \"harm\" is a strong word, Doctor. I wouldn't say they want to hurt me physically. I just... I don't want to sound paranoid, I really don't. But yes, I do feel like... like the routing system, the computer itself, is intentionally making things difficult for me. \n\nIt's just that it tracks every single move I make with the forklift, and then these messages come through the hand scanners. They aren't normal dispatch codes. They feel... pointed. Like it's trying to stress me out, make me mess up, so they have an excuse to push me out. You know, because I'm 62 and maybe they want someone younger and cheaper? \n\nI know it sounds like I'm just an old guy complaining about technology, right? I'm sorry if this sounds completely ridiculous. Do you think I'm just overreacting to normal workplace stress? I really try to be a good employee, I never cause trouble. I just... I can't shake the feeling that the system is targeting me specifically. Is that... is that something you've heard before from people?\n\nDoctor: Do you think the routing system's messages are actually intended for you, or could they be misinterpreted signals or codes?\n\nPatient: That's a really good question, Doctor. And believe me, I've tried to tell myself exactly that. I wanted it to just be a glitch, or maybe me just misreading the new software updates. I know I'm not the most tech-savvy guy, and I really don't want to be the kind of person who complains about things he just doesn't understand. \n\nBut... well, to be completely honest with you—and I hope you won't judge me too harshly for saying this—it just isn't a normal computer to me anymore. I... I know it has been marking my moves. My specific moves. And those messages coming through the scanners, I'm certain they're meant for me. It's doing it to push me out because I'm one of the old hands on the floor. \n\nI know how that sounds. Saying a warehouse routing system is after my job... it sounds terrible, doesn't it? I really don't want to come across as paranoid or difficult. I'm sorry if I'm making a fool of myself by saying all this. Do you think it's even possible for a system to be programmed that way, or... or am I just completely off base here?\n\nDoctor: How long have you believed the routing system was intentionally targeting you?\n\nPatient: Well... gosh, it's been a while now. I, um... I really hope I don't sound like I've been letting my imagination run wild for too long. I try so hard to just keep my head down and do my work, you know? \n\nBut if I'm being completely honest—and I want to be honest with you, Doctor—it's been going on for about... well, about a year and eight months. That's how long I've had these... these fixed beliefs about the scanners, and... and the voices, too. \n\nI know that's a long time to let something like this bother me. I probably should have said something sooner, right? I just didn't want to be a bother or have anyone think I couldn't handle my job anymore. I'm really sorry if I let it go on too long before getting help. Does that... does that timeframe make sense to you, Doctor? Am I giving you the right kind of information?\n\nDoctor: You mentioned voices, can you tell me more about what you mean by that?\n\nPatient: Oh... um... yes, I guess I did say that, didn't I? I... I really hesitate to even bring it up, because I know how it sounds. It makes me sound crazy, and I promise you, I'm really not. I'm just a normal guy who goes to work and comes home. \n\nBut... well, sometimes, even when I'm not at the warehouse... even when I'm just sitting in my own garage, you know, with everything shut off and it's perfectly quiet... I hear them. The dispatch voices. \n\nI try to ignore them, I really do. I tell myself it's just stress or my mind playing tricks on me after a long shift. But they sound as clear as day, Doctor. They... they call out my specific operator number. And they say... well, they say terrible things. That I'm dangerous, or that security is coming to get me. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that sounds incredibly alarming. Please don't think I'm a danger to anyone, I would never hurt a fly. It's just... it's very frightening, and I try my best to just push it away and not let it bother anyone else. Is it... is it okay that I'm telling you this? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need to help me.\n\nDoctor: Do you only hear the dispatch voices when you're near the warehouse equipment or can you hear them at any time, regardless of your location?\n\nPatient: Well... like I was just saying, it's not just at the warehouse. I really wish it was. If it was just at work, I could maybe tell myself it's the PA system acting up, or... or just the noise of the machinery playing tricks on my ears. I'd much prefer that, to be honest. \n\nBut... no, it happens when I'm far away from all that equipment, too. Like I mentioned, I can be sitting right there in my own garage, miles away from work. Everything is completely shut off, the door is down, and it's just... perfectly quiet. And then, out of nowhere, I hear them. Those same dispatch voices. \n\nThey're just as clear as day, Doctor. Even in my quiet garage, they call out my exact operator number. And... and they keep saying I'm dangerous, or that security is on their way to get me. \n\nI'm really trying my best not to let it interfere with my life, and I try to just keep quiet about it so I don't bother my neighbors or my family. I hope it's okay that I repeated that... I just want to make sure I'm answering your question right. Does that help clarify things for you, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that the routing system is not actually targeting you, and that your beliefs about it might be mistaken?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh... I mean, I know how it sounds. I really do. And I try to tell myself that it's just a computer, that I'm just being silly. I don't want to seem like I'm making a big fuss over nothing, or like I'm paranoid. I know you doctors hear all sorts of wild things, and I really don't want to be one of those difficult patients. \n\nBut... well, if I'm being completely honest—and I promised myself I'd be honest with you—it's just... it's more than a computer to me now. I know it sounds strange, but I can see it. It's marking my moves. It's sending these... these messages through the scanners. And I really believe it's trying to push me out. Just because I'm one of the old hands, you know? I've been there a long time, and I think they want me gone. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that probably sounds ridiculous. I really hope you don't think less of me for saying it. I just... I can't shake the feeling that it's real. Does that make sense? Am I explaining it okay, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can make the voices stop when you hear them?\n\nPatient: Oh... um, I really try to. I promise you I do. I don't want to be a burden, and I know I should probably be able to just... ignore it. \n\nBut... well, like I was saying a moment ago about being out in my garage... even when everything is completely shut off and I'm just trying to have a moment of peace, I just can't make them go away. I'll cover my ears or try to distract myself, but they stay clear as day. They just keep calling out my operator number, telling me I'm dangerous and that security is coming for me... and nothing I do makes it stop. \n\nI feel awful that I can't just snap out of it on my own. I really hope you don't think I'm weak for not being able to control it. Am I... is that answering your question okay, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voices are something you can influence or control in any way?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I know we just touched on this a second ago, and I am so sorry if I'm sounding like a broken record. I really don't want to waste your time, Doctor, or make you ask the same things twice. \n\nBut... no, I can't influence them at all. I try so hard, I really do. I'll be out there in the garage, like I mentioned, with the power totally cut and everything dead silent, just trying to focus my mind on a project or think about something pleasant. I try to mentally push it all away. \n\nBut those dispatch voices just cut right through whatever I'm doing. They still page my specific operator number, and they keep saying I'm a danger and that security is on the way to get me. It doesn't matter what I do, or how hard I try to change the subject in my head, or how much I try to reason with it... they just keep on. \n\nI'm so sorry I don't have a better answer for you, or that I can't just manage it better myself. I know I should be able to. Am I making sense, Doctor? I really hope I'm giving you the information you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any moments where you doubted that the routing system was intentionally targeting you?\n\nPatient: Oh, I... I know we sort of touched on this when we first started talking, and I am so sorry if I'm making you go over the same ground. I really don't want to be difficult or frustrate you. \n\nI try so hard to doubt it, Doctor. I really do. I tell myself I'm just being a foolish old man, that it's just a normal warehouse system and I'm letting my imagination run away with me. I want to be able to tell you that I know it's all in my head, because I really don't want you to think I'm crazy or unreasonable. \n\nBut... well, if I'm being completely honest, no, I haven't really been able to doubt it. Because it's just... it's not just a computer to me anymore. I can see exactly what it's doing. I know it's been marking my moves on the floor, and sending those specific messages through the scanners. It's all designed to push me out, just because I'm one of the old hands and they want to get rid of me. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know I shouldn't think that way, and I really wish I could just brush it off for you like a normal person would. I hope you don't think too badly of me for it. Did I... did I answer that right for you, Doctor? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any chance you could ever change your mind about the routing system targeting you?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I really, really wish I could say yes. I know I keep saying that, and I'm so sorry if I'm being stubborn. I don't want to be one of those difficult patients you read about. I want to be cooperative, I promise.\n\nI try to tell myself maybe I'll wake up tomorrow and it'll all just be... a misunderstanding. But... well, I'm here because that routing system at the warehouse... it just isn't just a computer to me anymore. I can't unsee what it's doing. I know it's been marking my moves on the floor, and... and sending those messages through the scanners. It's doing it to push me out, just because I'm one of the old hands. \n\nI'm so sorry, Doctor. I know that sounds awful, and I know I should be able to just change my mind and see reason. I feel terrible that I can't just give you the answer you're looking for. I hope I'm not disappointing you too much... is it okay that I'm being honest about this? I just want to make sure I'm doing this right for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices you hear are real?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... I know this probably sounds so silly, and I really don't want you to think I'm losing my mind entirely. I want to be a good patient and tell you that I know they aren't real, because I know that's what a sensible person would say. I really am trying to be sensible, Doctor.\n\nBut... well, yes. To me, they are real. I mean, even when I'm just sitting out in my garage, with the power totally cut and everything completely shut off... I still hear them. It's those dispatch voices, and they're just as clear as day. They call out my specific operator number, and they keep saying I'm dangerous, or that security is coming to get me. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that must sound completely ridiculous to you. I really wish I could tell you I knew it was just my imagination playing tricks on me. I feel awful admitting it because I don't want you to judge me or think I'm beyond help. Does that... does that make any sense at all, Doctor? I really hope I'm answering your questions the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can control your thoughts when you hear the voices?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... control my thoughts? I... I try to, Doctor. I really do. I don't want you to think I'm just letting them take over or that I'm not trying my hardest to stay focused. I know a strong person would just block them out, and I'm really trying to be strong for you.\n\nBut... when they start up, especially when they're saying my operator number and telling me those awful things about security... it's just so hard. It's like my brain just sort of... latches onto what they're saying, and I can't think about anything else. I try to distract myself, I really do, but it feels like they just crowd everything else out. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know I should be able to just snap out of it and think about something pleasant. I feel so weak admitting that I can't always control it. I hope you don't think I'm just giving up... I promise I'm trying. Is that... is that the kind of answer you needed, Doctor? I just want to be helpful.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

    "role": "user",

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, well... yes, Doctor. I... I really want to be completely honest with you, because I know you're just trying to help me, and I want to be a good patient. 

There was a time, a little while back, where the... the fears about the warehouse and the system were a bit quieter. I wasn't hearing the dispatch voices as much, and I thought... well, I really hoped I was finally getting better for you. I wanted so badly to come in and tell you that I was all fixed up.

But... oh, I feel so ashamed admitting this, because I know I should have just pulled myself up by my bootstraps when the voices faded. Even when the warehouse stuff wasn't bothering me as much... for most of this whole trouble, I've just been so... down. Just completely empty. 

I know a good man would just get back to his hobbies, but I just... I couldn't get myself back into fishing, or my woodworking out in the garage. I couldn't even bring myself to sit through family meals. I just felt so hollow. I'm so sorry, I know that makes me sound terribly lazy or ungrateful for my family. I really don't want you to think poorly of me. I tried to just snap out of it and be happy, I promise I did. Does that... does that make sense, Doctor? I hope I'm not disappointing you by admitting how low I've been.

That I'm dangerous, or that security is coming to get me. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that sounds incredibly alarming. Please don't think I'm a danger to anyone, I would never hurt a fly. It's just... it's very frightening, and I try my best to just push it away and not let it bother anyone else. Is it... is it okay that I'm telling you this? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need to help me.\n\nDoctor: Do you only hear the dispatch voices when you're near the warehouse equipment or can you hear them at any time, regardless of your location?\n\nPatient: Well... like I was just saying, it's not just at the warehouse. I really wish it was. If it was just at work, I could maybe tell myself it's the PA system acting up, or... or just the noise of the machinery playing tricks on my ears. I'd much prefer that, to be honest. \n\nBut... no, it happens when I'm far away from all that equipment, too. Like I mentioned, I can be sitting right there in my own garage, miles away from work. Everything is completely shut off, the door is down, and it's just... perfectly quiet. And then, out of nowhere, I hear them. Those same dispatch voices. \n\nThey're just as clear as day, Doctor. Even in my quiet garage, they call out my exact operator number. And... and they keep saying I'm dangerous, or that security is on their way to get me. \n\nI'm really trying my best not to let it interfere with my life, and I try to just keep quiet about it so I don't bother my neighbors or my family. I hope it's okay that I repeated that... I just want to make sure I'm answering your question right. Does that help clarify things for you, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that the routing system is not actually targeting you, and that your beliefs about it might be mistaken?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh... I mean, I know how it sounds. I really do. And I try to tell myself that it's just a computer, that I'm just being silly. I don't want to seem like I'm making a big fuss over nothing, or like I'm paranoid. I know you doctors hear all sorts of wild things, and I really don't want to be one of those difficult patients. \n\nBut... well, if I'm being completely honest—and I promised myself I'd be honest with you—it's just... it's more than a computer to me now. I know it sounds strange, but I can see it. It's marking my moves. It's sending these... these messages through the scanners. And I really believe it's trying to push me out. Just because I'm one of the old hands, you know? I've been there a long time, and I think they want me gone. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that probably sounds ridiculous. I really hope you don't think less of me for saying it. I just... I can't shake the feeling that it's real. Does that make sense? Am I explaining it okay, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can make the voices stop when you hear them?\n\nPatient: Oh... um, I really try to. I promise you I do. I don't want to be a burden, and I know I should probably be able to just... ignore it. \n\nBut... well, like I was saying a moment ago about being out in my garage... even when everything is completely shut off and I'm just trying to have a moment of peace, I just can't make them go away. I'll cover my ears or try to distract myself, but they stay clear as day. They just keep calling out my operator number, telling me I'm dangerous and that security is coming for me... and nothing I do makes it stop. \n\nI feel awful that I can't just snap out of it on my own. I really hope you don't think I'm weak for not being able to control it. Am I... is that answering your question okay, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voices are something you can influence or control in any way?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I know we just touched on this a second ago, and I am so sorry if I'm sounding like a broken record. I really don't want to waste your time, Doctor, or make you ask the same things twice. \n\nBut... no, I can't influence them at all. I try so hard, I really do. I'll be out there in the garage, like I mentioned, with the power totally cut and everything dead silent, just trying to focus my mind on a project or think about something pleasant. I try to mentally push it all away. \n\nBut those dispatch voices just cut right through whatever I'm doing. They still page my specific operator number, and they keep saying I'm a danger and that security is on the way to get me. It doesn't matter what I do, or how hard I try to change the subject in my head, or how much I try to reason with it... they just keep on. \n\nI'm so sorry I don't have a better answer for you, or that I can't just manage it better myself. I know I should be able to. Am I making sense, Doctor? I really hope I'm giving you the information you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any moments where you doubted that the routing system was intentionally targeting you?\n\nPatient: Oh, I... I really don't want to be difficult or frustrate you. \n\nI try so hard to doubt it, Doctor. I really do. Oh, um... other beliefs? Well, I... I really don't want to disappoint you, Doctor, but I don't have a whole bunch of strange ideas about everything else. I hope it's okay that I don't have anything new to report? I really try to stay grounded in reality for my family's sake. 

It really just comes back to what I was saying before. I know my family and the younger guys at work tell me I'm mistaken, that it's all in my head... but the whole reason I'm here is because that routing system at the warehouse just isn't a normal computer to me anymore. I've tried so hard to believe them when they say it's just software, but... I know it has been marking my moves. It sends these specific messages right through the hand scanners, just trying to push me out because I'm one of the old hands. 

I'm so sorry, I know saying that out loud probably makes me sound completely foolish. I hate that I can't just agree with everyone else and brush it off. I really don't want you to think I'm just being stubborn or refusing to listen to reason. I promise I'm not trying to be difficult. Does that... does that answer what you were asking, Doctor? I hope I'm not frustrating you by bringing it back to the warehouse again.

I know I'm not the most tech-savvy guy, and I really don't want to be the kind of person who complains about things he just doesn't understand. \n\nBut... well, to be completely honest with you—and I hope you won't judge me too harshly for saying this—it just isn't a normal computer to me anymore. I... I know it has been marking my moves. My specific moves. And those messages coming through the scanners, I'm certain they're meant for me. It's doing it to push me out because I'm one of the old hands on the floor. \n\nI know how that sounds. Saying a warehouse routing system is after my job... it sounds terrible, doesn't it? I really don't want to come across as paranoid or difficult. I'm sorry if I'm making a fool of myself by saying all this. Do you think it's even possible for a system to be programmed that way, or... or am I just completely off base here?\n\nDoctor: How long have you believed the routing system was intentionally targeting you?\n\nPatient: Well... gosh, it's been a while now. I, um... I really hope I don't sound like I've been letting my imagination run wild for too long. I try so hard to just keep my head down and do my work, you know? \n\nBut if I'm being completely honest—and I want to be honest with you, Doctor—it's been going on for about... well, about a year and eight months. That's how long I've had these... these fixed beliefs about the scanners, and... and the voices, too. \n\nI know that's a long time to let something like this bother me. I probably should have said something sooner, right? I just didn't want to be a bother or have anyone think I couldn't handle my job anymore. I'm really sorry if I let it go on too long before getting help. Does that... does that timeframe make sense to you, Doctor? Am I giving you the right kind of information?\n\nDoctor: You mentioned voices, can you tell me more about what you mean by that?\n\nPatient: Oh... um... yes, I guess I did say that, didn't I? I... I really hesitate to even bring it up, because I know how it sounds. It makes me sound crazy, and I promise you, I'm really not. I'm just a normal guy who goes to work and comes home. \n\nBut... well, sometimes, even when I'm not at the warehouse... even when I'm just sitting in my own garage, you know, with everything shut off and it's perfectly quiet... I hear them. The dispatch voices. \n\nI try to ignore them, I really do. I tell myself it's just stress or my mind playing tricks on me after a long shift. But they sound as clear as day, Doctor. They... they call out my specific operator number. And they say... well, they say terrible things. That I'm dangerous, or that security is coming to get me. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that sounds incredibly alarming. Please don't think I'm a danger to anyone, I would never hurt a fly. It's just... it's very frightening, and I try my best to just push it away and not let it bother anyone else. Is it... is it okay that I'm telling you this? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need to help me.\n\nDoctor: Do you only hear the dispatch voices when you're near the warehouse equipment or can you hear them at any time, regardless of your location?\n\nPatient: Well... like I was just saying, it's not just at the warehouse. I really wish it was. If it was just at work, I could maybe tell myself it's the PA system acting up, or... or just the noise of the machinery playing tricks on my ears. I'd much prefer that, to be honest. \n\nBut... no, it happens when I'm far away from all that equipment, too. Like I mentioned, I can be sitting right there in my own garage, miles away from work. Everything is completely shut off, the door is down, and it's just... perfectly quiet. And then, out of nowhere, I hear them. Those same dispatch voices. \n\nThey're just as clear as day, Doctor. Even in my quiet garage, they call out my exact operator number. And... and they keep saying I'm dangerous, or that security is on their way to get me. \n\nI'm really trying my best not to let it interfere with my life, and I try to just keep quiet about it so I don't bother my neighbors or my family. I hope it's okay that I repeated that... I just want to make sure I'm answering your question right. Does that help clarify things for you, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that the routing system is not actually targeting you, and that your beliefs about it might be mistaken?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh... I mean, I know how it sounds. I really do. And I try to tell myself that it's just a computer, that I'm just being silly. I don't want to seem like I'm making a big fuss over nothing, or like I'm paranoid. I know you doctors hear all sorts of wild things, and I really don't want to be one of those difficult patients. \n\nBut... well, if I'm being completely honest—and I promised myself I'd be honest with you—it's just... it's more than a computer to me now. I know it sounds strange, but I can see it. It's marking my moves. It's sending these... these messages through the scanners. And I really believe it's trying to push me out. Just because I'm one of the old hands, you know? I've been there a long time, and I think they want me gone. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that probably sounds ridiculous. I really hope you don't think less of me for saying it. I just... I can't shake the feeling that it's real. Does that make sense? Am I explaining it okay, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can make the voices stop when you hear them?\n\nPatient: Oh... um, I really try to. I promise you I do. I don't want to be a burden, and I know I should probably be able to just... ignore it. \n\nBut... well, like I was saying a moment ago about being out in my garage... even when everything is completely shut off and I'm just trying to have a moment of peace, I just can't make them go away. I'll cover my ears or try to distract myself, but they stay clear as day. They just keep calling out my operator number, telling me I'm dangerous and that security is coming for me... and nothing I do makes it stop. \n\nI feel awful that I can't just snap out of it on my own. I really hope you don't think I'm weak for not being able to control it. Am I... is that answering your question okay, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voices are something you can influence or control in any way?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I know we just touched on this a second ago, and I am so sorry if I'm sounding like a broken record. I really don't want to waste your time, Doctor, or make you ask the same things twice. \n\nBut... no, I can't influence them at all. I try so hard, I really do. I'll be out there in the garage, like I mentioned, with the power totally cut and everything dead silent, just trying to focus my mind on a project or think about something pleasant. I try to mentally push it all away. \n\nBut those dispatch voices just cut right through whatever I'm doing. They still page my specific operator number, and they keep saying I'm a danger and that security is on the way to get me. It doesn't matter what I do, or how hard I try to change the subject in my head, or how much I try to reason with it... they just keep on. \n\nI'm so sorry I don't have a better answer for you, or that I can't just manage it better myself. I know I should be able to. Am I making sense, Doctor? I really hope I'm giving you the information you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any moments where you doubted that the routing system was intentionally targeting you?\n\nPatient: Oh, I... I know we sort of touched on this when we first started talking, and I am so sorry if I'm making you go over the same ground. I really don't want to be difficult or frustrate you. \n\nI try so hard to doubt it, Doctor. I really do. I tell myself I'm just being a foolish old man, that it's just a normal warehouse system and I'm letting my imagination run away with me. I want to be able to tell you that I know it's all in my head, because I really don't want you to think I'm crazy or unreasonable. \n\nBut... well, if I'm being completely honest, no, I haven't really been able to doubt it. I know it's been marking my moves on the floor, and sending those specific messages through the scanners. It's all designed to push me out, just because I'm one of the old hands and they want to get rid of me. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know I shouldn't think that way, and I really wish I could just brush it off for you like a normal person would. I hope you don't think too badly of me for it. Did I... did I answer that right for you, Doctor? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any chance you could ever change your mind about the routing system targeting you?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I really, really wish I could say yes. I know I keep saying that, and I'm so sorry if I'm being stubborn. I don't want to be one of those difficult patients you read about. I want to be cooperative, I promise.\n\nI try to tell myself maybe I'll wake up tomorrow and it'll all just be... a misunderstanding. But... well, I'm here because that routing system at the warehouse... it just isn't just a computer to me anymore. I can't unsee what it's doing. I know it's been marking my moves on the floor, and... and sending those messages through the scanners. It's doing it to push me out, just because I'm one of the old hands. \n\nI'm so sorry, Doctor. I know that sounds awful, and I know I should be able to just change my mind and see reason. I feel terrible that I can't just give you the answer you're looking for. I hope I'm not disappointing you too much... is it okay that I'm being honest about this? I just want to make sure I'm doing this right for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices you hear are real?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... I know this probably sounds so silly, and I really don't want you to think I'm losing my mind entirely. I feel so weak admitting that I can't always control it. I hope you don't think I'm just giving up... I promise I'm trying. Is that... is that the kind of answer you needed, Doctor? I just want to be helpful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there are other things or people in your life that are also against you, aside from the routing system?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no... please don't think I'm completely gone, Doctor. I know how all this must sound, and I really hope you don't think I'm just sitting around suspecting everyone of terrible things. I promise I'm not like that. I try very hard to be a good person and get along with folks. \n\nIt's honestly just... just that system at the warehouse. Everyone else in my life... you know, my friends, the people in my neighborhood... they've all been perfectly fine to me. I... I know it has been marking my moves. My specific moves. And those messages coming through the scanners, I'm certain they're meant for me. I really don't want to come across as paranoid or difficult. I'm sorry if I'm making a fool of myself by saying all this. Do you think it's even possible for a system to be programmed that way, or... or am I just completely off base here?\n\nDoctor: How long have you believed the routing system was intentionally targeting you?\n\nPatient: Well... gosh, it's been a while now. I, um... I really hope I don't sound like I've been letting my imagination run wild for too long. I try so hard to just keep my head down and do my work, you know? \n\nBut if I'm being completely honest—and I want to be honest with you, Doctor—it's been going on for about... well, about a year and eight months. That's how long I've had these... these fixed beliefs about the scanners, and... and the voices, too. \n\nI know that's a long time to let something like this bother me. I probably should have said something sooner, right? I just didn't want to be a bother or have anyone think I couldn't handle my job anymore. I'm really sorry if I let it go on too long before getting help. Does that... does that timeframe make sense to you, Doctor? Am I giving you the right kind of information?\n\nDoctor: You mentioned voices, can you tell me more about what you mean by that?\n\nPatient: Oh... um... yes, I guess I did say that, didn't I? I... I really hesitate to even bring it up, because I know how it sounds. It makes me sound crazy, and I promise you, I'm really not. I'm just a normal guy who goes to work and comes home. \n\nBut... well, sometimes, even when I'm not at the warehouse... even when I'm just sitting in my own garage, you know, with everything shut off and it's perfectly quiet... I hear them. The dispatch voices. \n\nI try to ignore them, I really do. I tell myself it's just stress or my mind playing tricks on me after a long shift. But they sound as clear as day, Doctor. They... they call out my specific operator number. And they say... well, they say terrible things. That I'm dangerous, or that security is coming to get me. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that sounds incredibly alarming. Please don't think I'm a danger to anyone, I would never hurt a fly. It's just... it's very frightening, and I try my best to just push it away and not let it bother anyone else. Is it... is it okay that I'm telling you this? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need to help me.\n\nDoctor: Do you only hear the dispatch voices when you're near the warehouse equipment or can you hear them at any time, regardless of your location?\n\nPatient: Well... like I was just saying, it's not just at the warehouse. I really wish it was. If it was just at work, I could maybe tell myself it's the PA system acting up, or... or just the noise of the machinery playing tricks on my ears. I'd much prefer that, to be honest. \n\nBut... no, it happens when I'm far away from all that equipment, too. Like I mentioned, I can be sitting right there in my own garage, miles away from work. Everything is completely shut off, the door is down, and it's just... perfectly quiet. And then, out of nowhere, I hear them. Those same dispatch voices. \n\nThey're just as clear as day, Doctor. Even in my quiet garage, they call out my exact operator number. And... and they keep saying I'm dangerous, or that security is on their way to get me. \n\nI'm really trying my best not to let it interfere with my life, and I try to just keep quiet about it so I don't bother my neighbors or my family. I hope it's okay that I repeated that... I just want to make sure I'm answering your question right. Does that help clarify things for you, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that the routing system is not actually targeting you, and that your beliefs about it might be mistaken?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh... I mean, I know how it sounds. I really do. And I try to tell myself that it's just a computer, that I'm just being silly. I don't want to seem like I'm making a big fuss over nothing, or like I'm paranoid. I know you doctors hear all sorts of wild things, and I really don't want to be one of those difficult patients. \n\nBut... well, if I'm being completely honest—and I promised myself I'd be honest with you—it's just... it's more than a computer to me now. I know it sounds strange, but I can see it. It's marking my moves. It's sending these... these messages through the scanners. And I really believe it's trying to push me out. Just because I'm one of the old hands, you know? I've been there a long time, and I think they want me gone. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that probably sounds ridiculous. I really hope you don't think less of me for saying it. I just... I can't shake the feeling that it's real. Does that make sense? Am I explaining it okay, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can make the voices stop when you hear them?\n\nPatient: Oh... um, I really try to. I promise you I do. I don't want to be a burden, and I know I should probably be able to just... ignore it. \n\nBut... well, like I was saying a moment ago about being out in my garage... even when everything is completely shut off and I'm just trying to have a moment of peace, I just can't make them go away. I'll cover my ears or try to distract myself, but they stay clear as day. They just keep calling out my operator number, telling me I'm dangerous and that security is coming for me... and nothing I do makes it stop. \n\nI feel awful that I can't just snap out of it on my own. I really hope you don't think I'm weak for not being able to control it. Am I... is that answering your question okay, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voices are something you can influence or control in any way?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I know we just touched on this a second ago, and I am so sorry if I'm sounding like a broken record. I really don't want to waste your time, Doctor, or make you ask the same things twice. \n\nBut... no, I can't influence them at all. I try so hard, I really do. I'll be out there in the garage, like I mentioned, with the power totally cut and everything dead silent, just trying to focus my mind on a project or think about something pleasant. I try to mentally push it all away. \n\nBut those dispatch voices just cut right through whatever I'm doing. They still page my specific operator number, and they keep saying I'm a danger and that security is on the way to get me. It doesn't matter what I do, or how hard I try to change the subject in my head, or how much I try to reason with it... they just keep on. \n\nI'm so sorry I don't have a better answer for you, or that I can't just manage it better myself. I know I should be able to. Am I making sense, Doctor? I really hope I'm giving you the information you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any moments where you doubted that the routing system was intentionally targeting you?\n\nPatient: Oh, I... I know we sort of touched on this when we first started talking, and I am so sorry if I'm making you go over the same ground. I really don't want to be difficult or frustrate you. \n\nI try so hard to doubt it, Doctor. I really do. I tell myself I'm just being a foolish old man, that it's just a normal warehouse system and I'm letting my imagination run away with me. I want to be able to tell you that I know it's all in my head, because I really don't want you to think I'm crazy or unreasonable. \n\nBut... well, if I'm being completely honest, no, I haven't really been able to doubt it. I know it's been marking my moves on the floor, and sending those specific messages through the scanners. It's all designed to push me out, just because I'm one of the old hands and they want to get rid of me. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know I shouldn't think that way, and I really wish I could just brush it off for you like a normal person would. I hope you don't think too badly of me for it. Did I... did I answer that right for you, Doctor? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any chance you could ever change your mind about the routing system targeting you?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I really, really wish I could say yes. I know I keep saying that, and I'm so sorry if I'm being stubborn. I don't want to be one of those difficult patients you read about. I want to be cooperative, I promise.\n\nI try to tell myself maybe I'll wake up tomorrow and it'll all just be... a misunderstanding. But... well, I'm here because that routing system at the warehouse... it just isn't just a computer to me anymore. I can't unsee what it's doing. I know it's been marking my moves on the floor, and... and sending those messages through the scanners. It's doing it to push me out, just because I'm one of the old hands. \n\nI'm so sorry, Doctor. I know that sounds awful, and I know I should be able to just change my mind and see reason. I feel terrible that I can't just give you the answer you're looking for. I hope I'm not disappointing you too much... is it okay that I'm being honest about this? I just want to make sure I'm doing this right for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices you hear are real?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... I know this probably sounds so silly, and I really don't want you to think I'm losing my mind entirely. I want to be a good patient and tell you that I know they aren't real, because I know that's what a sensible person would say. I really am trying to be sensible, Doctor.\n\nBut... well, yes. It's those dispatch voices, and they're just as clear as day. They call out my specific operator number, and they keep saying I'm dangerous, or that security is coming to get me. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that must sound completely ridiculous to you. I hope I'm not disappointing you too much... is it okay that I'm being honest about this? I just want to make sure I'm doing this right for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices you hear are real?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... I know this probably sounds so silly, and I really don't want you to think I'm losing my mind entirely. I want to be a good patient and tell you that I know they aren't real, because I know that's what a sensible person would say. I really am trying to be sensible, Doctor.\n\nBut... well, yes. To me, they are real. I mean, even when I'm just sitting out in my garage, with the power totally cut and everything completely shut off... I still hear them. It's those dispatch voices, and they're just as clear as day. They call out my specific operator number, and they keep saying I'm dangerous, or that security is coming to get me. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that must sound completely ridiculous to you. I really wish I could tell you I knew it was just my imagination playing tricks on me. I feel awful admitting it because I don't want you to judge me or think I'm beyond help. And believe me, I've tried to tell myself exactly that. I wanted it to just be a glitch, or maybe me just misreading the new software updates. I know I'm not the most tech-savvy guy, and I really don't want to be the kind of person who complains about things he just doesn't understand. \n\nBut... well, to be completely honest with you—and I hope you won't judge me too harshly for saying this—it just isn't a normal computer to me anymore. I... I know it has been marking my moves. My specific moves. And those messages coming through the scanners, I'm certain they're meant for me. It's doing it to push me out because I'm one of the old hands on the floor. \n\nI know how that sounds. Saying a warehouse routing system is after my job... it sounds terrible, doesn't it? I really don't want to come across as paranoid or difficult. I'm sorry if I'm making a fool of myself by saying all this. Do you think it's even possible for a system to be programmed that way, or... or am I just completely off base here?\n\nDoctor: How long have you believed the routing system was intentionally targeting you?\n\nPatient: Well... gosh, it's been a while now. I, um... I really hope I don't sound like I've been letting my imagination run wild for too long. I try so hard to just keep my head down and do my work, you know? \n\nBut if I'm being completely honest—and I want to be honest with you, Doctor—it's been going on for about... well, about a year and eight months. That's how long I've had these... these fixed beliefs about the scanners, and... and the voices, too. \n\nI know that's a long time to let something like this bother me. I'm really sorry if I let it go on too long before getting help. Does that... does that timeframe make sense to you, Doctor? I'm just a normal guy who goes to work and comes home. \n\nBut... well, sometimes, even when I'm not at the warehouse... even when I'm just sitting in my own garage, you know, with everything shut off and it's perfectly quiet... I really hope you don't think I'm weak for not being able to control it. I'll be out there in the garage, like I mentioned, with the power totally cut and everything dead silent, just trying to focus my mind on a project or think about something pleasant. I try to mentally push it all away. \n\nBut those dispatch voices just cut right through whatever I'm doing. They still page my specific operator number, and they keep saying I'm a danger and that security is on the way to get me. I tell myself I'm just being a foolish old man, that it's just a normal warehouse system and I'm letting my imagination run away with me. I want to be able to tell you that I know it's all in my head, because I really don't want you to think I'm crazy or unreasonable. \n\nBut... well, if I'm being completely honest, no, I haven't really been able to doubt it. I really, really wish I could say yes. I know I keep saying that, and I'm so sorry if I'm being stubborn. I don't want to be one of those difficult patients you read about. I want to be cooperative, I promise.\n\nI try to tell myself maybe I'll wake up tomorrow and it'll all just be... a misunderstanding. But... well, I'm here because that routing system at the warehouse... it just isn't just a computer to me anymore. I can't unsee what it's doing. I really wish I could tell you I knew it was just my imagination playing tricks on me. I feel awful admitting it because I don't want you to judge me or think I'm beyond help. I try to, Doctor. I really do. I don't want you to think I'm just letting them take over or that I'm not trying my hardest to stay focused. I know a strong person would just block them out, and I'm really trying to be strong for you.\n\nBut... when they start up, especially when they're saying my operator number and telling me those awful things about security... it's just so hard. It's like my brain just sort of... latches onto what they're saying, and I can't think about anything else. I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need to help me.\n\nDoctor: Do you only hear the dispatch voices when you're near the warehouse equipment or can you hear them at any time, regardless of your location?\n\nPatient: Well... like I was just saying, it's not just at the warehouse. I really wish it was. If it was just at work, I could maybe tell myself it's the PA system acting up, or... or just the noise of the machinery playing tricks on my ears. I'd much prefer that, to be honest. \n\nBut... no, it happens when I'm far away from all that equipment, too. Like I mentioned, I can be sitting right there in my own garage, miles away from work. I really hope you don't think less of me for saying it. I just... I can't shake the feeling that it's real. Does that make sense? I'll cover my ears or try to distract myself, but they stay clear as day. They just keep calling out my operator number, telling me I'm dangerous and that security is coming for me... and nothing I do makes it stop. \n\nI feel awful that I can't just snap out of it on my own. I'll be out there in the garage, like I mentioned, with the power totally cut and everything dead silent, just trying to focus my mind on a project or think about something pleasant. I try to mentally push it all away. \n\nBut those dispatch voices just cut right through whatever I'm doing. They still page my specific operator number, and they keep saying I'm a danger and that security is on the way to get me. It doesn't matter what I do, or how hard I try to change the subject in my head, or how much I try to reason with it... they just keep on. \n\nI'm so sorry I don't have a better answer for you, or that I can't just manage it better myself.
